It’s probably not the worst thing, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should ask about it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep, even when you feel tired?\n\nPatient: Most days after my overnight shifts, I think. I’ll be really tired, so I feel like I should just fall asleep, but I end up lying there awake for a long time. And then when I finally do sleep, it’s kind of broken up… I wake up scared and like I didn’t really rest. I don’t know if that answers it right, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Outside of trying to sleep, do you find yourself feeling anxious or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Um… during the day, I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t think I walk around feeling panicky or anything like that. Maybe I get a little tense sometimes, but I kind of figure that’s normal with work and being tired. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes of intense fear or panic, especially during the night, where you wake up feeling extremely frightened or like something bad is happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like a full panic thing, but… yeah, at night, or when I’m trying to sleep after an overnight shift, I’ll finally drift off and then wake up really scared, like something’s wrong. I come in mostly because my sleep is just all broken up now. Even when I’m exhausted, I lie there wide awake, and then if I do sleep, it feels like I didn’t rest at all. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you wake up feeling scared during the night, do you notice your heart racing, sweating, or feeling like you can’t breathe, or is it more of a general sense of fear without those physical symptoms?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I notice all the body stuff every time… like I’m not sure about sweating or not breathing. It’s more that I wake up scared, and then I feel like I never really rested. It’s just my sleep is all broken up now—after an overnight shift I can be exhausted and still lie there wide awake, and then if I do drift off, I wake up scared. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you wake up feeling scared during the night, does your heart race, do you sweat, or do you feel like you can’t catch your breath?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I don’t really notice my heart or sweating like that… at least not enough to say for sure. It’s more like I wake up scared and confused for a minute. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up feeling scared at night, do you ever feel like your heart is pounding, you're sweating, or you're suddenly very short of breath?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I wake up scared, but I’m not sitting there thinking my heart is pounding or that I can’t breathe. Maybe I’m just not paying attention to it. Sorry, is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: During the day, have you ever had sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes out of nowhere, even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had it like that during the day… like, out of nowhere intense fear. I get uneasy sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. It’s not really like a sudden attack or anything, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: During the day, have you ever experienced sudden episodes of fear or discomfort that included physical symptoms like a pounding heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I don’t think I’ve had the pounding heart or sweating or not breathing during the day like that. I get uncomfortable sometimes, but not like a sudden physical episode. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up feeling as though you haven’t rested at all, even after you’ve been asleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, pretty often. I mean, I can get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’ll just lay there awake for a long time. And even when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared and it feels like I didn’t really sleep, like my body never rested. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up in the morning feeling as tired as when you went to bed, even if you managed to sleep for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I get a few hours, it doesn’t really feel like sleep helped. I’ll get off my overnight shift and still be lying there wide awake, and then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared and just… still tired, like I didn’t rest at all. I hope I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Even on the nights when you do manage to sleep for a few hours without waking up much, do you still wake up feeling as though you haven’t rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I don’t wake up a lot, I still don’t feel rested. It’s like I’ll get off my overnight shift and be so tired, but then I just lie there wide awake for a long time. And if I do sleep a few hours, I wake up feeling scared and like my body never really got any rest. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you wake up feeling scared during the night, does your heart race, do you sweat, or do you feel like you can’t catch your breath?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I don’t really notice my heart or sweating like that… at least not enough to say for sure. It’s more like I wake up scared and confused for a minute. I’m sorry, I know that’s probably not a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up feeling scared at night, do you ever feel like your heart is pounding, you're sweating, or you're suddenly very short of breath?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I wake up scared, but I’m not sitting there thinking my heart is pounding or that I can’t breathe. Maybe I’m just not paying attention to it. Sorry, is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: During the day, have you ever had sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes out of nowhere, even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had it like that during the day… like, out of nowhere intense fear. I get uneasy sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. It’s not really like a sudden attack or anything, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: During the day, have you ever experienced sudden episodes of fear or discomfort that included physical symptoms like a pounding heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I don’t think I’ve had the pounding heart or sweating or not breathing during the day like that. I get uncomfortable sometimes, but not like a sudden physical episode. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up feeling as though you haven’t rested at all, even after you’ve been asleep for a while?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, pretty often. I mean, I can get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’ll just lay there awake for a long time. And even when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared and it feels like I didn’t really sleep, like my body never rested. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up in the morning feeling as tired as when you went to bed, even if you managed to sleep for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I get a few hours, it doesn’t really feel like sleep helped. I’ll get off my overnight shift and still be lying there wide awake, and then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared and just… still tired, like I didn’t rest at all. I hope I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Even on the nights when you do manage to sleep for a few hours without waking up much, do you still wake up feeling as though you haven’t rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I don’t wake up a lot, I still don’t feel rested. It’s like I’ll get off my overnight shift and be so tired, but then I just lie there wide awake for a long time. And if I do sleep a few hours, I wake up feeling scared and like my body never really got any rest. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Even on the nights when you sleep for a few hours without waking up much, do you still wake up feeling as though you haven’t rested at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’m sorry, I know I kind of said this already, but yes. Even if I sleep a few hours and don’t wake up a bunch, I still wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. I get off my overnight shift exhausted, and then I’ll be lying there wide awake, and when I finally do drift off I wake up scared… like my body never really settled. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
